<pre>The question asks about the cofactor associated with a protein belonging to the FPP/GGPP synthase family.

Let me think about this:

1. FPP synthase (farnesyl diphosphate synthase) and GGPP synthase (geranylgeranyl diphosphate synthase) are prenyltransferases. These enzymes catalyze the condensation of dimethylallyl diphosphate (DMAPP) with isopentenyl diphosphate (IPP) to form farnesyl diphosphate (FPP) or geranylgeranyl diphosphate (GGPP).

2. These enzymes belong to the prenyltransferase superfamily, which includes the &quot;trans&quot; isoprenyl diphosphate synthases.

3. The catalytic mechanism of FPP/GGPP synthases involves the coordination of diphosphate groups from the substrates (allylic diphosphate and IPP). This coordination is achieved through divalent metal cations.

4. The cofactor for FPP/GGPP synthases is Mg²⁺ (magnesium ion). The enzyme typically uses three Mg²⁺ ions in the active site to coordinate the diphosphate groups of the substrates. These metal ions stabilize the negative charges of the diphosphate groups and facilitate the ionization of the allylic diphosphate, making it a better electrophile for the nucleophilic attack by the double bond of IPP.

5. Some prenyltransferases can also use Mn²⁺, but Mg²⁺ is the primary and physiological cofactor for FPP/GGPP synthases.

Looking at the protein sequence, it has features consistent with FPP/GGPP synthases:
- The &quot;DDXXD&quot; motif (aspartate-rich motif): I can see &quot;DIDVIERE&quot; and &quot;DIDDA&quot; and &quot;DILDF&quot; which contain the characteristic aspartate-rich motifs (DDXXD) that coordinate the Mg²⁺ ions.

Specifically, in the sequence:
- &quot;DDIDV&quot; - this contains the DDXXD motif
- &quot;DIDDA&quot; - another aspartate-rich region
- &quot;DILDF&quot; - contains aspartates

These aspartate-rich motifs are the hallmark of the metal (Mg²⁺) binding sites in prenyltransferases.

The answer is D. Mg²⁺.
